In the current folder, initialize a git repo and build a single-page web app that helps students visualize quadratic equations (y = ax² + bx + c). The app should have:

1. Interactive sliders for coefficients a (−5 to 5), b (−10 to 10), and c (−10 to 10) that update the graph in real time.
2. A canvas graph showing the parabola with a coordinate grid, labeled axes/ticks, and these visual elements:
- Red dot at the vertex with a coordinate label
- Blue dots at the x-intercepts (roots)
- Green dot at the y-intercept
- Grey dashed line for the axis of symmetry
3. A properties panel displaying: equation, vertex, axis of symmetry, direction, discriminant, and roots.
4. A tabbed "Step-by-Step Solutions" section with four tabs — Y-Intercept, Discriminant, Axis of Symmetry, and Quadratic Formula — each showing numbered steps that substitute the live coefficient values. Use KaTeX (from CDN) to render all math expressions with proper LaTeX notation (fractions, square roots, Greek letters, subscripts, boxed results). Tabs should be accessible with ARIA roles and keyboard arrow-key navigation.
5. Modern, responsive, accessible UI: CSS grid layout (sidebar + graph on desktop, stacked on mobile), dark mode via prefers-color-scheme, custom-styled sliders, and a reset button.
6. Graceful linear fallback when a = 0.
7. Bicep infrastructure-as-code (infra/main.bicep + infra/modules/staticWebApp.bicep) to provision a new resource group and a free-tier Azure Static Web App in westus2.

After creating all files and committing to git, deploy the Bicep template to my Azure subscription (I'm already logged into az cli), then push the web content to the new Static Web App using the SWA CLI.

```
👤 User

Can the static web app have a custom name that I supply?

🤖 Copilot

No — Azure Static Web Apps on the Free tier don't support custom domains with custom hostnames baked into the auto-generated URL. The subdomain (red-ground-0a3c6351e) is randomly assigned by Azure and can't be changed.

However, you have two options:

1. Custom domain (Free tier) — You can map your own domain (e.g., yolo2.example.com) via a CNAME record using az staticwebapp hostname set. You'd need to own a domain.

2. Upgrade to Standard tier — Still doesn't let you pick the auto-generated subdomain, but gives you more custom domain features.

Would you like to set up a custom domain?

👤 User

I'd like the chat history in the deployed web app to be easier to view rather than just being a flat text file. Specifically, I'd like to highlight messages from me, "User", and maybe have a way to make it easier to see the different steps you took. What do you suggest?

🤖 Copilot

Here's what I'd suggest:

1. Create a chat-history.html page that renders the chat history as a styled conversation view:
- User messages highlighted with a distinct left-border accent color (like blue)
- Copilot responses in a neutral card style
- Tool calls (sql, powershell, edit, etc.) collapsed into \<details\> accordions so you can expand them if curious, but they don't clutter the view
- Markdown rendering via a lightweight library (like marked.js from CDN) so code blocks, bold, lists render properly

2. Visual cues: avatars/icons (👤 for you, 🤖 for Copilot, ⚙️ for tool calls), timestamps if available, and alternating background shading.

3. Navigation: a sticky sidebar or top jump-links for each user message so you can quickly skip between the conversation turns.

4. Update the header link to point to chat-history.html instead of the raw .txt file.

Shall I build this, or would you prefer a different approach?
```

```
# GitHub Copilot CLI Documentation
## README
# GitHub Copilot CLI (Public Preview)
The power of GitHub Copilot, now in your terminal.
GitHub Copilot CLI brings AI-powered coding assistance directly to your command line, enabling you to build, debug, and understand code through natural language conversations. Powered by the same agentic harness as GitHub's Copilot coding agent, it provides intelligent assistance while staying deeply integrated with your GitHub workflow.
See [our official documentation](https://docs.github.com/copilot/concepts/agents/about-copilot-cli) for more information.
![Image of the splash screen for the Copilot CLI](https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/f40aa23d-09dd-499e-9457-1d57d3368887)
## 🚀 Introduction and Overview
We're bringing the power of GitHub Copilot coding agent directly to your terminal. With GitHub Copilot CLI, you can work locally and synchronously with an AI agent that understands your code and GitHub context.
- Terminal-native development: Work with Copilot coding agent directly in your command line — no context switching required.
- GitHub integration out of the box: Access your repositories, issues, and pull requests using natural language, all authenticated with your existing GitHub account.
- Agentic capabilities: Build, edit, debug, and refactor code with an AI collaborator that can plan and execute complex tasks.
- MCP-powered extensibility: Take advantage of the fact that the coding agent ships with GitHub's MCP server by default and supports custom MCP servers to extend capabilities.
- Full control: Preview every action before execution — nothing happens without your explicit approval.
We're still early in our journey, but with your feedback, we're rapidly iterating to make the GitHub Copilot CLI the best possible companion in your terminal.
## 📦 Getting Started
### Supported Platforms
- Linux
- macOS
- Windows
### Prerequisites
- (On Windows) PowerShell v6 or higher
- An active Copilot subscription. See [Copilot plans](https://github.com/features/copilot/plans?ref_cta=Copilot+plans+signup&ref_loc=install-copilot-cli&ref_page=docs).
If you have access to GitHub Copilot via your organization or enterprise, you cannot use GitHub Copilot CLI if your organization owner or enterprise administrator has disabled it in the organization or enterprise settings. See [Managing policies and features for GitHub Copilot in your organization](http://docs.github.com/copilot/managing-copilot/managing-github-copilot-in-your-organization/managing-github-copilot-features-in-your-organization/managing-policies-for-copilot-in-your-organization) for more information.
### Installation
Install with [WinGet](https://github.com/microsoft/winget-cli) (Windows):

bash
winget install GitHub.Copilot

bash
winget install GitHub.Copilot.Prerelease

Install with [Homebrew](https://formulae.brew.sh/cask/copilot-cli) (macOS and Linux):

bash
brew install copilot-cli

bash
brew install copilot-cli@prerelease

Install with [npm](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@github/copilot) (macOS, Linux, and Windows):

bash
npm install -g @github/copilot

bash
npm install -g @github/copilot@prerelease

Install with the install script (macOS and Linux):

bash
curl -fsSL https://gh.io/copilot-install | bash

Or

bash
wget -qO- https://gh.io/copilot-install | bash

Use | sudo bash to run as root and install to /usr/local/bin.
Set PREFIX to install to $PREFIX/bin/ directory. Defaults to /usr/local
when run as root or $HOME/.local when run as a non-root user.
Set VERSION to install a specific version. Defaults to the latest version.
For example, to install version v0.0.369 to a custom directory:

bash
curl -fsSL https://gh.io/copilot-install | VERSION="v0.0.369" PREFIX="$HOME/custom" bash

### Launching the CLI

bash
copilot

On first launch, you'll be greeted with our adorable animated banner! If you'd like to see this banner again, launch copilot with the --banner flag.
If you're not currently logged in to GitHub, you'll be prompted to use the /login slash command. Enter this command and follow the on-screen instructions to authenticate.
#### Authenticate with a Personal Access Token (PAT)
You can also authenticate using a fine-grained PAT with the "Copilot Requests" permission enabled.
1. Visit https://github.com/settings/personal-access-tokens/new
2. Under "Permissions," click "add permissions" and select "Copilot Requests"
3. Generate your token
4. Add the token to your environment via the environment variable GH_TOKEN or GITHUB_TOKEN (in order of precedence)
### Using the CLI
Launch copilot in a folder that contains code you want to work with.
By default, copilot utilizes Claude Sonnet 4.5. Run the /model slash command to choose from other available models, including Claude Sonnet 4 and GPT-5.
### Experimental Mode
Experimental mode enables access to new features that are still in development. You can activate experimental mode by:
- Launching with the --experimental flag: copilot --experimental
- Using the /experimental slash command from within the CLI
Once activated, the setting is persisted in your config, so the --experimental flag is no longer needed on subsequent launches.
#### Experimental Features
- Autopilot mode: Autopilot is a new mode (press Shift+Tab to cycle through modes), which encourages the agent to continue working until a task is completed.
Each time you submit a prompt to GitHub Copilot CLI, your monthly quota of premium requests is reduced by one. For information about premium requests, see [About premium requests](https://docs.github.com/copilot/managing-copilot/monitoring-usage-and-entitlements/about-premium-requests).
For more information about how to use the GitHub Copilot CLI, see [our official documentation](https://docs.github.com/copilot/concepts/agents/about-copilot-cli).
## 🔧 Configuring LSP Servers
GitHub Copilot CLI supports Language Server Protocol (LSP) for enhanced code intelligence. This feature provides intelligent code features like go-to-definition, hover information, and diagnostics.
### Installing Language Servers
Copilot CLI does not bundle LSP servers. You need to install them separately. For example, to set up TypeScript support:

bash
npm install -g typescript-language-server

For other languages, install the corresponding LSP server and configure it following the same pattern shown below.
### Configuring LSP Servers
LSP servers are configured through a dedicated LSP configuration file. You can configure LSP servers at the user level or repository level:
User-level configuration (applies to all projects):
Edit ~/.copilot/lsp-config.json
Repository-level configuration (applies to specific project):
Create .github/lsp.json in your repository root
Example configuration:

json
{
"lspServers": {
"typescript": {
"command": "typescript-language-server",
"args": ["--stdio"],
"fileExtensions": {
".ts": "typescript",
".tsx": "typescript"
}
}
}
}
`

### Viewing LSP Server Status

Check configured LSP servers using the /lsp command in an interactive session, or view your configuration files directly.

For more information, see the [changelog](./changelog.md).

## 📢 Feedback and Participation

We're excited to have you join us early in the Copilot CLI journey.

This is an early-stage preview, and we're building quickly. Expect frequent updates--please keep your client up to date for the latest features and fixes!

Your insights are invaluable! Open issue in this repo, join Discussions, and run /feedback` from the CLI to submit a confidential feedback survey!

## Help Command Output
Global shortcuts
@ - mention files, include contents in context
ctrl+x → / - run command (press ctrl+x, then /)
Esc - cancel the current operation
! - execute command in your local shell (bypass Copilot)
ctrl+c - cancel operation / clear input / exit
ctrl+d - shutdown
ctrl+l - clear the screen

Timeline shortcuts
ctrl+o - expand recent timeline (when no input)
ctrl+e - expand all timeline (when no input)
ctrl+t - toggle model reasoning display

Motion shortcuts
ctrl+a - move to beginning of line (when typing)
ctrl+e - move to end of line (when typing)
ctrl+h - delete previous character
ctrl+w - delete previous word
ctrl+u - delete from cursor to beginning of line
ctrl+k - delete from cursor to end of line
meta+←/→ - move cursor by word
↑ / ↓ - navigate command history

Available commands
/add-dir <directory> - Add a directory to the allowed list for file access
/agent - Browse and select from available agents (if any)
/allow-all, /yolo - Enable all permissions (tools, paths, and URLs)
/clear, /new - Clear the conversation history
/compact - Summarize conversation history to reduce context window usage
/context - Show context window token usage and visualization
/cwd, /cd [directory] - Change working directory or show current directory
/diff - Review the changes made in the current directory
/exit, /quit - Exit the CLI
/experimental [on|off|show] - Show available experimental features, or enable/disable experimental mode
/share [file|gist] [path] - Share session to markdown file or GitHub gist
/feedback - Provide feedback about the CLI
/help - Show help for interactive commands
/init - Initialize Copilot instructions and agentic features for this repository
/list-dirs - Display all allowed directories for file access
/login - Log in to Copilot
/logout - Log out of Copilot
/mcp [show|add|edit|delete|disable|enable] [server-name] - Manage MCP server configuration
/model, /models [model] - Select AI model to use
/plan [prompt] - Create an implementation plan before coding
/plugin [marketplace|install|uninstall|update|list] [args...] - Manage plugins and plugin marketplaces
/rename <name> - Rename the current session (alias for /session rename)
/reset-allowed-tools - Reset the list of allowed tools
/resume [sessionId] - Switch to a different session (optionally specify session ID)
/review [prompt] - Run code review agent to analyze changes
/session [checkpoints [n]|files|plan|rename <name>] - Show session info and workspace summary. Use subcommands for details.
/skills [list|info|add|remove|reload] [args...] - Manage skills for enhanced capabilities
/tasks - View and manage background tasks (subagents and shell sessions)
/terminal-setup - Configure terminal for multiline input support (shift+enter and ctrl+enter)
/theme [show|set|list] [auto|<theme-id>] - View or configure terminal theme
/usage - Display session usage metrics and statistics
/user [show|list|switch] - Manage GitHub user list

Copilot respects instructions from these locations:
CLAUDE.md
GEMINI.md
AGENTS.md (in git root & cwd)
.github/instructions/**/*.instructions.md (in git root & cwd)
.github/copilot-instructions.md
$HOME/.copilot/copilot-instructions.md
COPILOT_CUSTOM_INSTRUCTIONS_DIRS (additional directories via env var)

To learn about what I can do
Ask me: "What can you do?"
Or visit: https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/how-tos/use-copilot-agents/use-copilot-cli

You can also follow hyperlinks to related docs pages using the same API pattern.
````

```
🤖 Copilot

I see the problem. Both the body and the .main div have height: 100vh and overflow: hidden/overflow-y: auto, which creates nested scroll containers that don't work properly. The fix is to use CSS grid to let the layout handle heights properly and make the main content actually scroll all its content.
````

🤖 Copilot

The issue is that when clicking a sidebar anchor link (#user-43), the browser's default behavior is to scroll the closest scrollable ancestor to bring the target into view. But the .main div is the scroll container, and the browser may be scrolling the *entire page/body* instead, which is set to overflow: hidden. Let me also check if there's a problem with the anchor navigation breaking the layout.

Let me look at the actual HTML structure more carefully — the content blocks might have unclosed tags from the parser:

🤖 Copilot

There it is! 3 unclosed \<details\> tags and 186 instances of \<details\> inside \<p\> tags (invalid HTML — \<details\> is a block element that can't be inside \<p\>). The browser will auto-close the \<p\> when it encounters \<details\>, creating a mismatched DOM tree that breaks the layout.

Let me look at the parser to understand why this is happening:

⚙️ 1 tool call(s)
view

1. ﻿<#
2. .SYNOPSIS
3.   Parses yolo2-copilot-chat-history.txt and generates chat-history.html
4. #>
5. param(
6.   [string]$InputFile = ".\yolo2-copilot-chat-history.txt",
7.   [string]$OutputFile = ".\chat-history.html"
8. )
9.
10. $content = Get-Content $InputFile -Raw -Encoding UTF8
11.
12. # Split into sections by the --- separator
13. $sections = $content -split '(?m)^---\s*$'
14.
15. # Parse each section into typed blocks
16. $blocks = @()
17. $userIndex = 0
18.
19. foreach ($section in $sections) {
20.   $s = $section.Trim()
21.   if (-not $s) { continue }
22.
23.   if ($s -match '(?m)^### 👤 User') {
24.     $userIndex++
25.     $body = ($s -replace '(?m)^### 👤 User\s*', '').Trim()
26.     # Remove leading timestamp lines
27.     $body = ($body -replace '(?m)^<sub>.*?</sub>\s*', '').Trim()
28.     $blocks += @{ Type = 'user'; Body = $body; Index = $userIndex }
29.   }
30.   elseif ($s -match '(?m)^### 💬 Copilot') {
31.     $body = ($s -replace '(?m)^### 💬 Copilot\s*', '').Trim()
32.     $body = ($body -replace '(?m)^<sub>.*?</sub>\s*', '').Trim()
33.     $blocks += @{ Type = 'copilot'; Body = $body }
34.   }
35.   elseif ($s -match '(?m)^### [✅❌⚠️🔧] (.+?)') {
36.     $toolName = $Matches[1]
37.     $body = $s -replace '(?m)^### [✅❌⚠️🔧] .+?\s*', ''
38.     $body = ($body -replace '(?m)^<sub>.*?</sub>\s*', '').Trim()
39.     $blocks += @{ Type = 'tool'; Tool = $toolName; Body = $body }
40.   }
41.   elseif ($s -match '(?m)^# 🤖 Copilot CLI Session') {
42.     $blocks += @{ Type = 'header'; Body = $s }
43.   }
44.   else {
45.     # Timestamp-only or other content
46.     if ($s -match '<sub>') { continue }
47.     $blocks += @{ Type = 'other'; Body = $s }
48.   }
49. }
50.
51. # HTML-escape helper
52. function HtmlEncode($text) {
53.   return [System.Net.WebUtility]::HtmlEncode($text)
54. }
55.
56. # Simple markdown to HTML (bold, code blocks, inline code, lists)
57. function MarkdownToHtml($text) {
58.   if (-not $text) { return '' }
59.   $t = $text
60.
61.   # Fenced code blocks (

...`)
62. $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '(?ms)`(\w*)\r?\n(.*?)`', {
63. param($m)
64. $lang = $m.Groups[1].Value
65. $code = [System.Net.WebUtility]::HtmlEncode($m.Groups[2].Value.TrimEnd())
66. "<pre><code class="language-$lang">$code</code></pre>"
67. })
68.
69. # <details> blocks - pass through as-is
70. # Already HTML, leave them alone
71.
72. # Inline code
73. $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '([^]+?)', '<code>$1</code>')
74.
75. # Bold
76. $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '\*\*(.+?)\*\*', '<strong>$1</strong>')
77.
78. # Line breaks for paragraphs (double newline)
79. $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '(\r?\n){2,}', '</p><p>')
80.
81. # Single line breaks within paragraphs
82. $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '(?<!</p>)\r?\n(?!<)', '<br/>')
83.
84. return "<p>$t</p>"
85. }
86.
87. # Build the user message jump nav
88. $userMessages = $blocks | Where-Object { $_.Type -eq 'user' }
89.
90. $navHtml = ""
91. $msgNum = 0
92. foreach ($um in $userMessages) {
93. $msgNum++
94. $preview = $um.Body
95. if ($preview.Length -gt 60) { $preview = $preview.Substring(0, 60) + "..." }
96. $preview = [System.Net.WebUtility]::HtmlEncode($preview)
97. $navHtml += " <a href="#user-$msgNum" class="nav-link">$msgNum. $preview</a>n"
98. }
99.
100. # Build body blocks
101. $bodyHtml = ""
102. $userCount = 0
103. $toolGroup = @()
104. $inToolGroup = $false
105.
106. function FlushToolGroup {
107. $script:toolGroupHtml = ""
108. if ($script:toolGroup.Count -eq 0) { return "" }
109. $html = "<div class="tool-group"><details><summary>⚙️ $($script:toolGroup.Count) tool call(s)</summary>"
110. foreach ($tg in $script:toolGroup) {
111. $toolLabel = [System.Net.WebUtility]::HtmlEncode($tg.Tool)
112. $toolBody = MarkdownToHtml $tg.Body
113. $html += "<div class="tool-call"><div class="tool-name">$toolLabel</div><div class="tool-body">$toolBody</div></div>"
114. }
115. $html += "</details></div>"
116. $script:toolGroup = @()
117. return $html
118. }
119.
120. foreach ($block in $blocks) {
121. if ($block.Type -eq 'tool') {
122. $toolGroup += $block
123. $inToolGroup = $true
124. continue
125. }
126.
127. # Flush any pending tool group
128. if ($inToolGroup) {
129. $bodyHtml += FlushToolGroup
130. $inToolGroup = $false
131. }
132.
133. switch ($block.Type) {
134. 'header' {
135. $hBody = MarkdownToHtml $block.Body
136. $bodyHtml += "<div class="block header-block">$hBody</div>"
137. }
138. 'user' {

```
🤖 Copilot

Now I understand the root problems:

1. \<details\> inside \<p\> is invalid HTML — the browser auto-closes \<p\> when it sees a block element, creating DOM tree corruption. The MarkdownToHtml function wraps everything in \<p\> tags, including lines that contain \<details\>.

2. 3 unclosed \<details\> tags — the markdown content from tool outputs contains raw \<details\>/\<summary\> HTML that the parser doesn't handle correctly. When MarkdownToHtml converts newlines to \<br/\> and wraps in \<p\>, it breaks the \<details\> structure.

3. No anchor scroll handling — clicking sidebar links uses default browser hash navigation which may scroll the wrong container.

Let me fix the MarkdownToHtml function to handle \<details\> blocks properly, and add proper anchor scroll JS:

57. function MarkdownToHtml($text) {
58.   if (-not $text) { return '' }
59.   $t = $text
60.
61.   # Fenced code blocks (

...`)
62. $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '(?ms)`(\w*)\r?\n(.*?)`', {
63. param($m)
64. $lang = $m.Groups[1].Value
65. $code = [System.Net.WebUtility]::HtmlEncode($m.Groups[2].Value.TrimEnd())
66. "n<pre><code class="language-$lang">$code</code></pre>n"
67. })
68.
69. # Extract <details>...</details> blocks to prevent them from being wrapped in <p>
70. $detailsPlaceholders = @{}
71. $detailsCounter = 0
72. $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '(?ms)<details>.*?</details>', {
73. param($m)
74. $key = "%%DETAILS_${script:detailsCounter}%%"
75. $script:detailsPlaceholders[$key] = $m.Value
76. $script:detailsCounter++
77. "n$keyn"
78. })
79.
80. # Extract <pre>...</pre> blocks that were already created above
81. $prePlaceholders = @{}
82. $preCounter = 0
83. $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '(?ms)<pre><code.*?</code></pre>', {
84. param($m)
85. $key = "%%PRE_${script:preCounter}%%"
86. $script:prePlaceholders[$key] = $m.Value
87. $script:preCounter++
88. "n$keyn"
89. })
90.
91. # Inline code
92. $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '([^]+?)', '<code>$1</code>')
93.
94. # Bold
95. $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '\*\*(.+?)\*\*', '<strong>$1</strong>')
96.
97. # Split into paragraphs on double newlines, process each
98. $paragraphs = [regex]::Split($t, '(\r?\n){2,}')
99. $result = ""
100. foreach ($para in $paragraphs) {
101. $p = $para.Trim()
102. if (-not $p) { continue }
103. # If it's a placeholder, output it directly (not wrapped in <p>)
104. if ($p -match '^%%DETAILS_\d+%%$') {
105. $result += $detailsPlaceholders[$p]
106. }
107. elseif ($p -match '^%%PRE_\d+%%$') {
108. $result += $prePlaceholders[$p]
109. }
110. else {
111. # Restore any inline placeholders within mixed content
112. foreach ($key in $detailsPlaceholders.Keys) {
113. $p = $p.Replace($key, "</p>$($detailsPlaceholders[$key])<p>")
114. }
115. foreach ($key in $prePlaceholders.Keys) {
116. $p = $p.Replace($key, "</p>$($prePlaceholders[$key])<p>")
117. }
118. # Convert single newlines to <br/>
119. $p = [regex]::Replace($p, '\r?\n', '<br/>')
120. $result += "<p>$p</p>"
````

⚙️ 1 tool call(s)
powershell

Test MarkdownToHtml function

<details>
<summary>Arguments</summary>

{
"command": "cd C:\\github\\bretstateham\\yolo2; powershell -NoProfile -Command {\n  function MarkdownToHtml($text) {\n    if (-not $text) { return '' }\n    $t = $text\n    $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '(?ms)

(\\w*)\\r?\\n(.*?)`', { param($m); $lang = $m.Groups[1].Value; $code = [System.Net.WebUtility]::HtmlEncode($m.Groups[2].Value.TrimEnd()); \"n<pre><code class=\"language-$lang\">$code</code></pre>n\" })\n $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '([^]+?)', '<code>$1</code>')\n $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '\\*\\*(.+?)\\*\\*', '<strong>$1</strong>')\n $lines = $t -split '\\r?\\n'\n $result = \"\"\n $paraLines = @()\n function FlushPara {\n if ($script:paraLines.Count -gt 0) {\n $content = ($script:paraLines -join '<br/>').Trim()\n if ($content) { $script:result += \"<p>$content</p>\" }\n $script:paraLines = @()\n }\n }\n foreach ($line in $lines) {\n $trimmed = $line.Trim()\n if (-not $trimmed) { FlushPara; continue }\n if ($trimmed -match '^<(pre|details|summary|/details|/summary|/pre)') { FlushPara; $result += $trimmed; continue }\n $paraLines += $trimmed\n }\n FlushPara\n $result = $result -replace '<p>\\s*</p>', ''\n return $result\n }\n $test = \"You need to give me the exact syntax I need to use to save the chat history file\"\n $r = MarkdownToHtml $test\n Write-Host \"Result: [$r]\"\n Write-Host \"Length: $($r.Length)\"\n}",
"description": "Test MarkdownToHtml function"
}

</details>

Result: []
Length: 0
<exited with exit code 0>
``

🤖 Copilot

The function returns empty! The problem is $script:paraLines and $script:result — inside a nested function (FlushPara) within another function (MarkdownToHtml), $script: doesn't refer to the outer function's scope, it refers to the actual script scope. Let me look at how FlushPara accesses the variables:

The issue is clear: FlushPara uses $script:paraLines and $script:result, but these are *local* variables to MarkdownToHtml. In PowerShell, a nested function can't access the enclosing function's locals via $script: — that points to the script-level scope. The variables $paraLines and $result in MarkdownToHtml are in the function scope, not the script scope.

I need to avoid the nested function pattern entirely:

⚙️ 1 tool call(s)
view

view the file at C:\github\bretstateham\yolo2\build-chat-html.ps1.

C:\github\bretstateham\yolo2\build-chat-html.ps1 (lines 57-110)

<details>
<summary>54 lines</summary>

57. function MarkdownToHtml($text) {
58.   if (-not $text) { return '' }
59.   $t = $text
60.
61.   # Fenced code blocks (

...`)
62. $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '(?ms)`(\w*)\r?\n(.*?)`', {
63. param($m)
64. $lang = $m.Groups[1].Value
65. $code = [System.Net.WebUtility]::HtmlEncode($m.Groups[2].Value.TrimEnd())
66. "n<pre><code class="language-$lang">$code</code></pre>n"
67. })
68.
69. # Inline code (before splitting)
70. $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '([^]+?)', '<code>$1</code>')
71.
72. # Bold
73. $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '\*\*(.+?)\*\*', '<strong>$1</strong>')
74.
75. # Process line-by-line to keep block elements out of <p> tags
76. $lines = $t -split '\r?\n'
77. $result = ""
78. $paraLines = @()
79.
80. function FlushPara {
81. if ($script:paraLines.Count -gt 0) {
82. $content = ($script:paraLines -join '<br/>').Trim()
83. if ($content) {
84. $script:result += "<p>$content</p>"
85. }
86. $script:paraLines = @()
87. }
88. }
89.
90. foreach ($line in $lines) {
91. $trimmed = $line.Trim()
92. # Empty line = paragraph break
93. if (-not $trimmed) {
94. FlushPara
95. continue
96. }
97. # Block-level HTML elements should not be inside <p>
98. if ($trimmed -match '^<(pre|details|summary|/details|/summary|/pre)') {
99. FlushPara
100. $result += $trimmed
101. continue
102. }
103. # Accumulate inline content
104. $paraLines += $trimmed
105. }
106. FlushPara
107.
108. # Clean up empty <p></p> tags
109. $result = $result -replace '<p>\s*</p>', ''
110.
````

</details>

const toggle = document.getElementById('sidebar-toggle');

});

layout.classList.add('collapsed');

// Scroll target into view within the .main scroll container
function scrollToTarget(id, smooth) {
const target = document.getElementById(id);
if (!target || !mainEl) return;
const mainRect = mainEl.getBoundingClientRect();
const targetRect = target.getBoundingClientRect();
const scrollOffset = targetRect.top - mainRect.top + mainEl.scrollTop - 16;
mainEl.scrollTo({ top: scrollOffset, behavior: smooth ? 'smooth' : 'auto' });
}
// Handle sidebar nav clicks
document.querySelectorAll('.nav-link').forEach(link => {
link.addEventListener('click', (e) => {
e.preventDefault();
const id = link.getAttribute('href').slice(1);
scrollToTarget(id, true);
history.replaceState(null, '', '#' + id);
});
});
// Handle initial hash on page load
if (location.hash) {
requestAnimationFrame(() => scrollToTarget(location.hash.slice(1), false));
}
</script>

🤖 Copilot

The JS looks correct. But wait — the user says "same issue" which was the sidebar disappearing. The only way the sidebar disappears is if layout.classList gets collapsed added. The only code that does that is the toggle click handler and the mobile width check.

But the original problem was about the browser's default anchor navigation. When navigating via URL hash (#user-43), the browser performs its own scroll BEFORE the JS runs. This can cause the browser to scroll the body or html element, which with overflow: hidden would clip the layout.

🤖 Copilot

I think the core problem might be simpler than I thought. When the browser loads #user-43 directly in the URL, it performs its own scroll-into-view on the anchor element. Since .main has overflow-y: auto, the browser correctly scrolls .main. But the issue could be that the browser ALSO scrolls the body or .layout element, since overflow: hidden doesn't prevent programmatic scroll-into-view for anchor targets.

Let me try a completely different approach: don't use IDs on the content blocks at all for the hash targets. Instead, use a data attribute and handle all navigation purely in JS. This prevents the browser from doing any default anchor scrolling:

327.     // Scroll target into view within the .main scroll container
328.     function scrollToMsg(msgId, smooth) {
329.       const target = document.querySelector('[data-msg="' + msgId + '"]');
330.       if (!target || !mainEl) return;
331.       const mainRect = mainEl.getBoundingClientRect();
332.       const targetRect = target.getBoundingClientRect();
333.       const scrollOffset = targetRect.top - mainRect.top + mainEl.scrollTop - 16;
334.       mainEl.scrollTo({ top: scrollOffset, behavior: smooth ? 'smooth' : 'auto' });
335.     }
336.     // Handle sidebar nav clicks
337.     document.querySelectorAll('.nav-link').forEach(link => {
338.       link.addEventListener('click', (e) => {
339.         e.preventDefault();
340.         const msgId = link.getAttribute('href').slice(1);
341.         scrollToMsg(msgId, true);
342.         history.replaceState(null, '', '#' + msgId);
343.       });
344.     });

🤖 Copilot

There it is! Look at this in the generated content:

\</details\>\<br/\>\</code\>

\</p\>\</div\>\</div\>\</details\>\</div\>
``

There are extra \</div\>\</div\>\</details\>\</div\> tags in the content that are closing the tool-call, tool-group, AND the .main div! The source chat history text contains raw \</details\> and \</div\> tags that the MarkdownToHtml function passes through literally, and those close the parent containers.

The fix is simple: HTML-encode the content of tool call bodies so raw \</div\> and \</details\>` in the source text don't break the DOM. Let me check where tool body content flows through:

⚙️ 1 tool call(s)

135. function FlushToolGroup {
136.   $script:toolGroupHtml = ""
137.   if ($script:toolGroup.Count -eq 0) { return "" }
138.   $html = "<div class="tool-group"><details><summary>⚙️ $($script:toolGroup.Count) tool call(s)</summary>"
139.   foreach ($tg in $script:toolGroup) {
140.     $toolLabel = [System.Net.WebUtility]::HtmlEncode($tg.Tool)
141.     $toolBody = MarkdownToHtml $tg.Body
142.     $html += "<div class="tool-call"><div class="tool-name">$toolLabel</div><div class="tool-body">$toolBody</div></div>"
143.   }
144.   $html += "</details></div>"
145.   $script:toolGroup = @()
146.   return $html
````

```
🤖 Copilot

Now I see it clearly. The tool body content has line numbers (from the chat export) mixed with raw HTML like \<details\>, \</details\>, \<summary\>, ---. The MarkdownToHtml function treats lines starting with \<details\> and \</details\> as block-level and passes them through. But these are from content TEXT (the chat export format), not from intentional HTML structure.

The root cause: the tool body text contains the raw text of the chat export format which includes \<details\>, \</details\>, \<sub\>, ---, ### markers. These get interpreted as HTML by the MarkdownToHtml function.

The fix: tool body content should be HTML-encoded first, then only apply code block formatting. The MarkdownToHtml function should NOT pass through raw HTML for tool bodies.

🤖 Copilot

The tool sections include \<details\>\<summary\>Arguments\</summary\> which is part of the chat export format. The MarkdownToHtml function then sees these as block elements and passes them through, but the \</details\> in content closes the parent \<details\> of the tool-group wrapper.

The simplest fix: HTML-encode the tool body content before running MarkdownToHtml, but preserve fenced code blocks. Actually even simpler: just make the tool body use a safe rendering that HTML-encodes everything except what MarkdownToHtml explicitly creates:

⚙️ 1 tool call(s)
view

view the file at C:\github\bretstateham\yolo2\build-chat-html.ps1.

<details>
<summary>59 lines</summary>

57. function MarkdownToHtml($text) {
58.   if (-not $text) { return '' }
59.   $t = $text
60.
61.   # Fenced code blocks (

...`)
62. $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '(?ms)`(\w*)\r?\n(.*?)`', {
63. param($m)
64. $lang = $m.Groups[1].Value
65. $code = [System.Net.WebUtility]::HtmlEncode($m.Groups[2].Value.TrimEnd())
66. "n<pre><code class="language-$lang">$code</code></pre>n"
67. })
68.
69. # Inline code (before splitting)
70. $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '([^]+?)', '<code>$1</code>')
71.
72. # Bold
73. $t = [regex]::Replace($t, '\*\*(.+?)\*\*', '<strong>$1</strong>')
74.
75. # Process line-by-line to keep block elements out of <p> tags
76. $lines = $t -split '\r?\n'
77. $result = ""
78. $paraLines = [System.Collections.ArrayList]@()
79.
80. foreach ($line in $lines) {
81. $trimmed = $line.Trim()
82. # Empty line = paragraph break — flush accumulated inline lines
83. if (-not $trimmed) {
84. if ($paraLines.Count -gt 0) {
85. $content = ($paraLines -join '<br/>').Trim()
86. if ($content) { $result += "<p>$content</p>" }
87. $paraLines.Clear()
88. }
89. continue
90. }
91. # Block-level HTML elements should not be inside <p>
92. if ($trimmed -match '^<(pre|details|summary|/details|/summary|/pre)') {
93. if ($paraLines.Count -gt 0) {
94. $content = ($paraLines -join '<br/>').Trim()
95. if ($content) { $result += "<p>$content</p>" }
96. $paraLines.Clear()
97. }
98. $result += $trimmed
99. continue
100. }
101. # Accumulate inline content
102. [void]$paraLines.Add($trimmed)
103. }
104. # Final flush
105. if ($paraLines.Count -gt 0) {
106. $content = ($paraLines -join '<br/>').Trim()
107. if ($content) { $result += "<p>$content</p>" }
108. }
109.
110. # Clean up empty <p></p> tags
111. $result = $result -replace '<p>\s*</p>', ''
112.
113. return $result
114. }
115.
````

</details>

🤖 Copilot

The problem is clear. The function takes raw text with \<details\>, \</details\>, \<sub\>, etc. and passes them through as HTML. The fix: HTML-encode the entire text first, then apply markdown transformations on the encoded text. This way no raw HTML from the source can break the DOM.
